Yeah, hi… I’m here because things at work have been getting, um, really obvious lately. I drive routes, and dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there are these little comments over the radio that line up too closely with what I’m doing. Like, it’s not just normal check-ins or paperwork stuff.

People keep saying, “Oh, that’s just oversight,” or “They track everybody,” and I understand that’s what it’s supposed to look like, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s the way they say things, the timing, the little remarks after I change something or take a different route. Um… I don’t think so. Not like voices, no. I mean, I hear the radio, and I hear people talking when I’m around the yard or at stops, but that’s actual people, like there’s a source for it.

So… yeah, about two months that I’ve been noticing it in a way I can’t really brush off.\n\nDoctor: Besides the radio comments you can actually hear, have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people around you couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like voices, no. I mean, I hear the radio, and I hear people talking when I’m around the yard or at stops, but that’s actual people, like there’s a source for it.\n\nSometimes I’ll replay stuff in my head afterward, like I keep thinking about a comment someone made and what they meant by it, but that’s more me thinking about it, not like a voice out in the room. And with the truck, there are always noises—engine sounds, beeping, static, the scanner, all that—so sometimes I’ll double-check what I heard. But I can’t say I’ve heard a voice that nobody else could hear, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two months, have you been using any alcohol, marijuana, stimulants, or other drugs, including any changes in how much or how often?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, nothing really that I can point to as, like, a change. I’m trying to think because with work schedules, sometimes days blur together, and I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nI don’t really use drugs or anything like that. Alcohol is not a big thing for me—if I have a drink it’s more like socially or with dinner, not something I’m doing every day or to get through the day. And marijuana, stimulants… no, not like that. I haven’t started anything new or been taking more of anything over these past couple months.\n\nI know people ask because that can affect how someone’s seeing things or reacting to things, and I get that. But this didn’t start because I was partying or taking something. There hasn’t been some big change like that.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two months, have you also noticed changes in your mood—like feeling unusually sad, hopeless, or on the flip side, unusually energetic, elated, or needing much less sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve felt, like, unusually happy or full of energy or anything like that. I haven’t had that feeling where I’m on top of the world or don’t need sleep because I’m so energized.\n\nSad or hopeless… I don’t know. I’ve been stressed, definitely, and frustrated, and sometimes worn down by all of it, because when you feel like things at work are lining up in a certain way it’s hard not to carry that home with you. But I don’t know if I’d call it depression exactly. It’s more like tense, on edge, thinking things over too much.\n\nSleep has been kind of up and down, but not because I feel amazing or have tons of energy. More like my mind doesn’t settle right away. But I haven’t noticed some big mood swing where I’m suddenly a totally different person, if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly… not as usual, no. I wish I could say I’m keeping up, because I’ve always been pretty organized at work, but lately I’m falling behind on things I normally handle without making a big production out of it.

Like the route reports—I keep meaning to finish them, and then I get stuck going back over details or checking things, and suddenly I’m behind. And when I’m giving instructions, I guess they’ve been coming out more confusing than I realize, because drivers have complained. That part is embarrassing, because I’m not trying to make anyone’s job harder, and I don’t think of myself as someone who can’t explain a route or a change clearly. But lately it’s like I’ll think I’ve said it plainly, and then somebody comes back irritated saying they didn’t understand what I meant.

At home it’s kind of showing too. My husband has been picking up a lot of the slack. Meals, phone calls, just basic planning—stuff like what needs to get done, who needs to be contacted, what we’re doing that evening. I’ll either put it off or get overwhelmed trying to sort it out, and then he just does it because otherwise it doesn’t happen. I don’t like admitting that. It makes me feel like I’m not handling normal adult things the way I should be.

Yeah… if I’m being honest, it didn’t just appear out of nowhere two months ago. The worst of it has been recently, but looking back, I think it’s been building for maybe eight months or so.

It kind of lines up with when they put me in that temporary dispatch lead role. At the time I thought it was just the stress of having more responsibility, because suddenly people were coming to me for answers and I felt like I had to watch everything more closely. But then I started feeling like there were things going on underneath what people were saying, like I needed to pay closer attention to tone or little comments. I don’t know if I would’ve described it as “suspicious” back then, exactly, because it felt more like I was trying to stay on top of the job and not mess up.

But yeah, six months to a year ago, there were earlier versions of it. Milder. Like I was more guarded, less comfortable around coworkers, not as relaxed with people as I used to be. And I probably did pull back some, though at the time I told myself I was just tired or focused on work. It’s only now, looking back, that I can see it was sort of starting then.

But I can’t say I’ve heard a voice that nobody else could hear, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two months, have you been using any alcohol, marijuana, stimulants, or other drugs, including any changes in how much or how often?\n\nPatient: Um… I mean, nothing really that I can point to as, like, a change. I’m trying to think because with work schedules, sometimes days blur together, and I don’t want to answer wrong.\n\nI don’t really use drugs or anything like that. Alcohol is not a big thing for me—if I have a drink it’s more like socially or with dinner, not something I’m doing every day or to get through the day. And marijuana, stimulants… no, not like that. I haven’t started anything new or been taking more of anything over these past couple months.\n\nI know people ask because that can affect how someone’s seeing things or reacting to things, and I get that. But this didn’t start because I was partying or taking something. There hasn’t been some big change like that.\n\nDoctor: Over these past two months, have you also noticed changes in your mood—like feeling unusually sad, hopeless, or on the flip side, unusually energetic, elated, or needing much less sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve felt, like, unusually happy or full of energy or anything like that. I haven’t had that feeling where I’m on top of the world or don’t need sleep because I’m so energized.\n\nSad or hopeless… I don’t know. I’ve been stressed, definitely, and frustrated, and sometimes worn down by all of it, because when you feel like things at work are lining up in a certain way it’s hard not to carry that home with you. But I don’t know if I’d call it depression exactly. It’s more like tense, on edge, thinking things over too much.\n\nSleep has been kind of up and down, but not because I feel amazing or have tons of energy. More like my mind doesn’t settle right away. But I haven’t noticed some big mood swing where I’m suddenly a totally different person, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Outside of these concerns about being watched or talked about, how are things going otherwise—are you keeping up with your job performance, your relationships, and daily routines like usual?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not as usual, no. I wish I could say I’m keeping up, because I’ve always been pretty organized at work, but lately I’m falling behind on things I normally handle without making a big production out of it.\n\nLike the route reports—I keep meaning to finish them, and then I get stuck going back over details or checking things, and suddenly I’m behind. And when I’m giving instructions, I guess they’ve been coming out more confusing than I realize, because drivers have complained. That part is embarrassing, because I’m not trying to make anyone’s job harder, and I don’t think of myself as someone who can’t explain a route or a change clearly. But lately it’s like I’ll think I’ve said it plainly, and then somebody comes back irritated saying they didn’t understand what I meant.\n\nAt home it’s kind of showing too. My husband has been picking up a lot of the slack. Meals, phone calls, just basic planning—stuff like what needs to get done, who needs to be contacted, what we’re doing that evening. I’ll either put it off or get overwhelmed trying to sort it out, and then he just does it because otherwise it doesn’t happen. I don’t like admitting that. It makes me feel like I’m not handling normal adult things the way I should be.\n\nDoctor: Before these past two months, going back further—say six months to a year ago—did you notice any earlier, milder versions of feeling suspicious of people, sensing hidden meanings in things, or pulling back from coworkers and friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… if I’m being honest, it didn’t just appear out of nowhere two months ago. The worst of it has been recently, but looking back, I think it’s been building for maybe eight months or so.\n\nIt kind of lines up with when they put me in that temporary dispatch lead role. At the time I thought it was just the stress of having more responsibility, because suddenly people were coming to me for answers and I felt like I had to watch everything more closely. But then I started feeling like there were things going on underneath what people were saying, like I needed to pay closer attention to tone or little comments. I don’t know if I would’ve described it as “suspicious” back then, exactly, because it felt more like I was trying to stay on top of the job and not mess up.\n\nBut yeah, six months to a year ago, there were earlier versions of it. Milder. Like I was more guarded, less comfortable around coworkers, not as relaxed with people as I used to be. And I probably did pull back some, though at the time I told myself I was just tired or focused on work. It’s only now, looking back, that I can see it was sort of starting then."

Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\n{\"key\":\"None\",\"name\":\"None\",\"description\":\"None\",\"reason\":\"The doctor is asking about auditory hallucinations—hearing voices or sounds others cannot hear—but the provided symptom profile does not include a hallucinations item.\"}\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
  }

    "S034": "The patient denies feeling full of energy or having tons of energy.",
    "S036": "The patient denies not needing sleep because of being energized."
  }
}

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\n{\"key\":\"functional_impairment\",\"name\":\"Functional Impairment\",\"description\":\"required\",\"reason\":\"The doctor is asking whether symptoms are affecting job performance, relationships, and daily routines, which directly corresponds to functional impairment.\"}\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
